    whitespace differences never destabilise an assertion.
  - `internal/testutil/normalize.go` (new) — `NormalizeRequestID`,
    `NormalizeTraceID`, `NormalizeDuration`, `NormalizeTimestamps`,
    `ChainNormalizers`, `DefaultJSONNormalizers`. The default stack
    handles every dynamic field yalla currently emits.
  - `internal/testutil/httpfix.go` (new) — `RoundTripperFunc`,
    `JSONResponse`, `NewServer`, `RecordingServer`, `Recorder`,
    `CapturedRequest` (with `AsJSON`), `AssertOperationCalled`, and
    `LookupOperation`. The recording layer reads `r.Body`, restores
    it for the inner handler, and then exposes the raw bytes — that
    means contract tests can drive a single fixture for both the wire
    assertion and the response logic.
  - `internal/testutil/cli_test.go`, `golden_test.go`,
    `normalize_test.go`, `httpfix_test.go` (new) — exercise every
    helper in both the success and failure branches via a `fakeT`
    stub that satisfies `testing.TB` through embedding. The golden
    tests `chdir` into a temp directory so they never write to the
    real `testdata/` directory and never depend on a checked-in fixture.
  - `internal/testutil/AGENTS.md` (new) — module-local conventions and
    the explicit "don't" list (no production imports, no
    `IsolateEnv` bypass, no direct testdata writes).
  - `ralph/VERIFICATION.md` (new) — focused vs full verification
    loops, the per-command contract checklist, and the API operation
    contract recipe future stories will follow.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — set US-0008 `passes: true`.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean (no module change; `internal/testutil` only
    imports first-party packages).
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all six packages pass: `internal/api`,
    `internal/cli`, `internal/config`, `internal/errors`,
    `internal/output`, `internal/testutil` (new).
  - `go test -race ./...` — same.
  - Smoke: `go test -count=1 ./internal/testutil/ -update-golden`
    succeeds. The `TestGolden_UpdateModeWritesFile` covers the write
    branch and the comparison-branch tests opt out via
    `SetUpdateGoldenForTest(t, false)` so update-mode runs do not
    mask logic regressions.
- **Tools NOT available locally** (per the verification policy):
  `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`,
  `goreleaser`. Same baseline as US-0001 through US-0007.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The harness is purposely consumed only from external `_test`
    packages (its own tests use `package testutil_test`). Existing
    in-package tests under `internal/cli/` and `internal/api/` keep
    their local helpers (`runRootArgs`, `roundTripperFunc`, …) so
    we did not churn passing tests. New stories should reach for
    `testutil` first; refactoring older tests is fair game in a
    later cleanup.
  - `Golden` writes with mode `0644` and the testdata directory
    with `0755`. That is the standard Go convention but worth
    revisiting if we ever ship goldens with secrets baked in (we
    should not — keep secrets out of goldens entirely).
  - The `fakeT` shim used inside the testutil tests cannot stop
    execution on `Fatalf` (only the real `testing.T` can). Helpers
    we test must therefore be safe to keep running after a Fatal —
    a useful constraint that ruled out a couple of "panic on
    impossible state" shortcuts during implementation.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **Cobra + test binaries**: `cmd.SetArgs(nil)` falls back to
    `os.Args[1:]`. Inside a `go test` binary that means the test
    binary's own flags (`-update-golden`, `-test.run`, …) leak into
    the command tree. Always pass an explicit slice — even an
    empty one — when constructing a command for a test.
  - **`testing.TB` embedding**: structs that embed `testing.TB`
    satisfy the interface despite the unexported `private()`
    method. That made the `fakeT` shim possible without dropping
    out of the `testing.TB` parameter type for every helper.
  - **Flag registration timing**: `flag.Bool` at package init is the
    right hook for `-update-golden`; `testing.M` calls
    `flag.Parse()` once on its own, so we never call it ourselves.
    Tests that want to override the value mid-run reach for
    `SetUpdateGoldenForTest` instead of `flag.Set` so the cleanup
    path is consistent.
  - **Net-test isolation**: `httptest.NewServer` plus `t.Setenv` is
    the simplest way to wire `YALLA_BASE_URL`/`YALLA_TOKEN` for the
    raw API executor. The recording variant must read the request
    body BEFORE forwarding it (and restore it via
    `io.NopCloser(bytes.NewReader(b))`) so the inner handler still
    sees the original bytes.
  - **Verification doc placement**: `ralph/VERIFICATION.md` lives
    next to the PRD so future iterations grep `ralph/` for guidance
    and find the focused-vs-full loop in one hop.
---
## 2026-05-08 - US-0009
- **Implemented**: the production release pipeline. GoReleaser config covers
  every supported target (linux/darwin/windows × amd64/arm64), publishes
  GitHub Releases with sha256 checksums, generates a Homebrew tap formula,
  Scoop manifest, and a WinGet PR, and ships an npm/npx wrapper that downloads
  + verifies + execs the matching native binary while preserving argv, stdio,
  and exit codes. Two GitHub Actions workflows (CI and Release) automate the
  loop. A new `internal/release` Go package centralises the build matrix and
  validates the goreleaser config, the npm wrapper, and the workflows agree
  on the same `<os>_<arch>` coordinates.
- **Files changed**:
  - `LICENSE` (new) — MIT license referenced by goreleaser archive `files:`,
    Homebrew formula, Scoop manifest, WinGet manifest, and npm package.json.
  - `README.md` (new) — install matrix, usage examples, link to RELEASING.md.
  - `.gitignore` (new) — ignore `dist/`, `npm/binaries/`, etc.
  - `.goreleaser.yaml` (new) — schema v2 config with builds, archives,
    checksums, snapshot template, GitHub Release config, brews, scoops, and
    winget channels. Each package-manager channel uses `skip_upload: auto`
    plus an env-default block so missing tokens or fork PRs never abort.
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new) — gofmt + go mod tidy + go vet +
    `go test` + `go test -race` matrix on ubuntu/macos/windows, plus
    `goreleaser check` and `goreleaser release --snapshot --clean
    --skip=publish` on Linux with snapshot artefact upload.
  - `.github/workflows/release.yml` (new) — tag-driven `goreleaser release
    --clean` followed by an npm publish job that stamps
    `npm/package.json#version` from the tag (`vX.Y.Z` → `X.Y.Z`).
  - `npm/package.json` (new) — wrapper manifest with `bin.yalla = bin/yalla.js`,
    `postinstall = node install.js`, dependency-free contract.
  - `npm/lib/platform.js` (new) — `OS_MAP`, `ARCH_MAP`, `detect`,
    `archive(version)`, `checksumName`, `parseChecksum`. Source of truth for
    the npm side of the build matrix.
  - `npm/lib/install.js` (new) — zero-dep downloader: `https.get` with
    redirect handling and 60s timeout, sha256 verification against the
    GoReleaser checksums file, extraction via `tar -xf` with a
    PowerShell `Expand-Archive` fallback for Windows. Version resolution:
    `YALLA_VERSION` env > `package.json#version`.
  - `npm/install.js` (new) — postinstall script. Honors
    `YALLA_SKIP_DOWNLOAD=1`. Swallows network failures (exits 0) so the
    wrapper retries on first run instead of breaking `npm install`.
  - `npm/bin/yalla.js` (new) — the trampoline. `child_process.spawn(bin,
    process.argv.slice(2), { stdio: 'inherit' })`, re-raises terminating
    signals, exits with the binary's exit code.
  - `npm/test/wrapper.test.js` (new) — `node --test` covers the platform
    map, archive name builder, and `parseChecksum` (hex normalisation +
    starred-binary lines + missing-filename error). All 8 tests pass under
    Node 20.
  - `npm/README.md`, `npm/AGENTS.md`, `npm/.npmignore` (new) — usage doc,
    agent guidance, and publish hygiene.
  - `internal/release/release.go` (new) — `SupportedTargets`, `Target`,
    `Target.ArchiveExt`, `Target.BinaryName`, `Target.ArchiveName`,
    `ChecksumsName`. Public API for downstream tooling (US-0010 will
    consume this for upgrade detection).
  - `internal/release/release_test.go` (new) — six tests:
    `TestGoReleaserConfig_CoversEveryTarget`,
    `TestGoReleaserConfig_ArchivesAndChecksums`,
    `TestGoReleaserConfig_PackageManagerChannels`,
    `TestArchiveNamesMatchGoReleaserTemplate`,
    `TestNPMWrapperPackage`,
    `TestNPMWrapperPlatformMappingMatchesGo`,
    `TestReleaseWorkflowsExist`.
    Walks up to `go.mod` via `runtime.Caller` to locate the project root,
    so a future package move only fails one place.
  - `internal/release/AGENTS.md` (new) — module-local conventions.
  - `RELEASING.md` (new) — channel matrix, workflow map, secret table,
    release checklist.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — set US-0009 `passes: true`.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -l .` — clean (gofmt -w canonicalised release_test.go's struct
    tag alignment on the first run).
  - `go mod tidy` — clean (no module change; `internal/release` only
    imports the existing `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` dependency).
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass: `internal/api`, `internal/cli`,
    `internal/config`, `internal/errors`, `internal/output`,
    `internal/release` (new, 6 tests), `internal/testutil`.
  - `go test -race ./...` — same.
  - `cd npm && node --test test` — 8/8 pass under Node 20.19.2.
- **Tools NOT available locally**: `goreleaser`, `goimports`, `golangci-lint`,
  `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`. The CI workflow invokes `goreleaser check`
  and a `--snapshot --skip=publish` dry-run on every push/PR so the config
  is exercised end-to-end before any release tag.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The Homebrew tap (`JuribaDev/homebrew-yalla`), Scoop bucket
    (`JuribaDev/scoop-yalla`), and WinGet token are not provisioned yet.
    `skip_upload: auto` plus the env-default block makes this graceful, but
    the first real release will need those repos created and the matching
    secrets added to GitHub Actions.
  - The npm `yalla-cli` package name is unclaimed — a release will fail at
    publish time if someone squats it. We can pivot to a scoped name
    (`@juribadev/yalla-cli`) by editing `npm/package.json#name` only; the
    bin entry and trampoline contract stay identical.
  - `YALLA_VERSION` env override is supported but undocumented in the main
    CLI help. US-0010 (channel-aware upgrade) will probably surface it.
  - The wrapper extracts archives using `tar -xf` (Windows tar.exe handles
    zip via bsdtar). On older Windows builds without tar.exe, the
    PowerShell fallback runs. There's no third-party JS unzip dep — keep
    it that way.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **GoReleaser v2 schema**: archives use `formats: [tar.gz]` (plural,
    array). `format` (singular) silently parses but produces no archive.
    `format_overrides` follows the same shape. Modeling the YAML in a
    Go test with `KnownFields(false)` lets us assert the keys we care
    about without fighting the rest of the schema.
  - **Token guards**: setting `skip_upload: auto` on every brew/scoop/winget
    channel plus an `env:` block that defaults the token vars to
    `placeholder` makes a fork PR's snapshot dry-run pass without secrets.
    The release workflow still uses the real tokens via
    `${{ secrets.* }}`.
  - **Cross-platform extraction**: the Windows-10+ `tar.exe` accepts zip
    archives transparently, so `tar -xf <archive> -C <dir>` is the single
    extraction command for both `.tar.gz` and `.zip`. PowerShell
    `Expand-Archive` only fires on the rare missing-tar.exe case.
  - **Node child stdio**: `spawn(bin, args, { stdio: 'inherit' })` is the
    only correct trampoline pattern. Anything that pipes through Node
    (e.g. `spawnSync` returning buffers) silently breaks the
    "stdout-is-data-only" agent contract on the first command that
    emits binary or non-UTF8 bytes.
  - **Project-root discovery in tests**: `runtime.Caller(0)` + walking
    parents until `go.mod` is found beats hard-coding `../..`. Tests stay
    valid through package moves and survive `go test ./...` invocations
    from any working directory.
  - **npm `os` / `cpu` fields**: declaring the supported tuples in
    `package.json` lets npm refuse the install on truly unsupported
    platforms before postinstall runs, so the user sees a clean error
    instead of a wrapper-side "unsupported platform" crash. Keep it in
    sync with `release.SupportedTargets`.
  - **Checksum file format**: GoReleaser writes `<hex>  <filename>` per
    line, sometimes with a leading `*` (binary mode flag from coreutils).
    `parseChecksum` strips the `*` and lowercases the hex so the wrapper
    survives both shapes.
---

## 2026-05-08 - US-0010
- **Implemented**: Channel-aware upgrade commands. `yalla upgrade --check`
  detects the install channel (Homebrew, npm, npx, Scoop, WinGet, manual,
  unknown) without spawning subprocesses, fetches the latest release from
  the GitHub Releases API, and reports either the package-manager command
  or — for manual installs — `yalla upgrade --yes` to download and replace
  the binary atomically. `--check`, `--check --json`, and `--yes` are all
  scriptable; `--check`/`--yes` are mutually exclusive; bare `yalla
  upgrade` is `--check` (the safe default).
- **Files added**:
  - `internal/upgrade/detect.go` — `Channel`, `Probe`, `Detect`,
    `DefaultProbe`. Pure path-pattern + env-driven classifier with no
    side effects.
  - `internal/upgrade/plan.go` — `Plan` + `PlanFor(channel, binaryPath)`.
    Maps channel → package-manager command + Note. Only `ChannelManual`
    is `SelfUpgradable: true`.
  - `internal/upgrade/version.go` — minimal `SemVer`, `ParseSemVer`, and
    `Compare`. Implements SemVer 2.0 §11 ordering for pre-release
    identifiers without pulling an external dep.
  - `internal/upgrade/check.go` — `LatestRelease`, `ReleaseAsset`,
    `CheckOptions`, `Check(ctx, opts)`. Talks to GitHub via injected
    `*http.Client` + `BaseURL`; supports `IncludePrerelease`.
  - `internal/upgrade/apply.go` — `ApplyOptions`, `ApplyManual(ctx, opts)`.
    Downloads checksums + archive, verifies SHA-256, extracts the binary
    (tar.gz on Unix, zip on Windows), and atomically renames over the
    on-disk binary. Aborts before any write on checksum mismatch.
  - `internal/cli/upgrade_cmd.go` — Cobra command. Owns the JSON
    envelope (`upgradeStatusDoc`), exit-code mapping, and the
    `upgradeCheckBaseURL` / `upgradeApplyArchiveURL` /
    `upgradeApplyChecksums` / `upgradeProbeFactory` test seams.
  - `internal/upgrade/AGENTS.md` — package conventions doc.
  - Tests: `detect_test.go`, `version_test.go`, `check_test.go`,
    `apply_test.go`, `upgrade_cmd_test.go`. Coverage spans every
    install-method decision branch (Homebrew, npm, npx, Scoop, WinGet,
    manual on Linux/Windows, unknown), version comparison cases,
    successful download/replace, checksum-mismatch refusal,
    missing-checksum-entry refusal, Windows zip extraction, command-
    level JSON envelopes per channel, the `--yes` refusal on
    package-managed channels, network-error mapping, mutual exclusion
    of `--check` / `--yes`, and a `--no-input` smoke test.
- **Files modified**:
  - `internal/cli/root.go` — registers `newUpgradeCommand()`.
  - `internal/cli/manifest_cmd_test.go::wantTopLevel` — added `upgrade`.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — `US-0010.passes = true`.
  - `ralph/progress.txt` — Codebase Patterns + this report.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -l .` → clean
  - `go vet ./...` → clean
  - `go mod tidy` → no changes
  - `go test ./...` → ok (every package, including
    `internal/upgrade` and `internal/cli`)
  - `go test -race ./...` → ok
  - `goimports`, `staticcheck`, `golangci-lint`, `govulncheck`,
    `goreleaser` → not installed locally; skipped (consistent with
    every prior story).
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - Production-only branches not covered by unit tests:
    - The non-prerelease GitHub `/releases/latest` endpoint is HTTPS
      to api.github.com; if the user is rate-limited (HTTP 403) we
      surface it as a generic non-2xx string. A future US could add
      a `Retry-After`-aware backoff and a `CodeRateLimited` mapping
      for upgrade checks.
    - `ApplyManual` does not currently sign the archive: SHA-256
      from the GoReleaser `checksums.txt` is the only integrity
      guarantee. US-0011 (security/dependency verification gates)
      should layer cosign or PGP verification on top.
  - Detection is path-based. A future "force channel" flag
    (e.g. `--channel manual`) would let an operator override it on
    a corporate-package-manager layout that yalla cannot
    auto-detect; not implemented to keep US-0010 minimal.
  - The Linux-temp-dir path in `TestUpgradeYes_ManualChannel_*`
    deliberately ends in `/bin/yalla` so `Detect` classifies it as
    Manual. If a future detection refactor narrows the manual
    heuristic, this fixture needs updating.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **Channel detection without subprocesses**: every install method
    has a stable path-segment marker. Homebrew → `Cellar/yalla/`,
    npm → `node_modules/yalla-cli/`, npx → `_npx/`, Scoop →
    `\scoop\apps\yalla\`, WinGet → `\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages\` or
    `\WindowsApps\`. Substring matching is hermetic, fast, and CI-
    friendly; shelling out to `brew --prefix yalla` would be
    flakier and slower for no real gain.
  - **HTTP test seams**: package-level vars (`upgradeCheckBaseURL`,
    `upgradeApplyArchiveURL`, etc.) restored via `t.Cleanup` are
    cleaner than threading dependency-injection structs through the
    Cobra layer for a single command. Mirrors US-0005's
    `apiCallClientFactory` pattern; both are documented in the
    `internal/cli` AGENTS.md so future commands reuse the seam.
  - **Atomic replace order**: download → verify → write to sibling
    temp file → rename. Doing the verification BEFORE writing the
    temp file (or any byte to disk near the binary) means a
    tampered archive cannot leave a partially-replaced binary
    behind. The `defer` in `atomicReplace` only removes the temp
    file if the rename failed (checked via `os.Stat`).
  - **GoReleaser checksums format compatibility**: `lookupChecksum`
    uses `strings.Fields` so it tolerates one or many spaces; we
    also strip a leading `*` per coreutils' binary-mode flag — the
    same shape the npm wrapper's `parseChecksum` accepts.
  - **Per-OS archive format**: `internal/release.Target.ArchiveExt`
    is `.zip` on Windows and `.tar.gz` everywhere else, and
    `extractBinary` branches on the same. Reusing `release.Target`
    (instead of duplicating the table inside `internal/upgrade`)
    keeps the build matrix honest in one place.
  - **Don't auto-update silently**: `internal/upgrade` is imported
    only from `internal/cli/upgrade_cmd.go`. No other command
    triggers an upgrade check. The "never auto-update silently"
    PRD requirement is an architectural property, not a runtime
    flag.
---

## 2026-05-08 - US-0011
- **Implemented**: Security and dependency verification gates. Layered the
  vulnerability scan, lint suite, and dependency-review action on top of the
  existing commit gate; added a single local entry point (`scripts/verify.sh`)
  that mirrors the CI pipeline; documented the minimum required checks for
  commit and release in `SECURITY.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md`; added an
  end-to-end secret-leak regression net that drives the production
  exit-code path with a sentinel token across every redaction-relevant
  rendering path.
- **Files changed**:
  - `scripts/verify.sh` (new) — required + optional checks, `--release`,
    `--strict`, `--quiet` flags. Reports missing optional tools explicitly
    rather than silently skipping.
  - `.golangci.yml` (new) — configured linter set: `govet`, `staticcheck`,
    `errcheck`, `ineffassign`, `unused`, `bodyclose`, `gosec` (G304
    excluded), `errorlint`, `gofmt`, `goimports`, `misspell`, `unconvert`,
    `revive`. Test files relax cosmetic linters; `internal/api/data/`
    relaxes verbosity linters for verbatim payloads.
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — added a `security` job that runs
    `govulncheck`, `staticcheck`, and `golangci-lint` on every push and
    PR. Existing test/goreleaser jobs unchanged.
  - `.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml` (new) —
    `actions/dependency-review-action@v4` on every PR with
    `fail-on-severity: high` and an explicit OSI license allowlist.
  - `SECURITY.md` (new) — vulnerability disclosure process, threat model,
    redaction contract, required verification gates table, dependency
    review pointer, and a best-effort disclosure timeline.
  - `CONTRIBUTING.md` (new) — minimum required checks before commit,
    minimum required checks before release, optional-tool install hints,
    coding conventions, dependency-review checklist, and a Ralph Loop
    pointer.
  - `README.md` — added Security and Contributing sections linking the
    new docs.
  - `internal/cli/redaction_security_test.go` (new) — the canonical
    end-to-end secret-leak regression net.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — `US-0011.passes` flipped to `true`.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all pass (cli 1.106s, race 2.656s, total all green).
  - `go test -race ./...` — all pass.
  - `bash scripts/verify.sh --quiet` — exit 0; reports the four optional
    tools as missing (govulncheck, staticcheck, golangci-lint, goreleaser)
    rather than silently skipping.
  - `govulncheck ./...` — installed and ran locally; surfaced two
    pre-existing stdlib advisories tied to the host toolchain
    (`go1.26.2` vs fixed `go1.26.3`). See "Remaining risks" below — the
    point of US-0011 was to wire the gate, not to bump the toolchain.
- **Results**: All required checks pass. The new secret-leak suite has 6
  top-level test cases (some table-driven with sub-cases) — every one
  passes. The lint and vulnerability gates are live in CI starting with
  the next push.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The local `govulncheck` run found two stdlib vulns (`GO-2026-4971`,
    `GO-2026-4918`) reported against `net@go1.26.2` (fixed in
    `net@go1.26.3`). These exist on the developer host's toolchain. CI
    installs Go via `setup-go` with `go-version-file: go.mod` (currently
    `go 1.24`), which should pick up the latest 1.24.x patch — fixes for
    both advisories are typically backported, but the CI run will be the
    authoritative answer. If CI flags the same advisories, the
    follow-up is to add a `toolchain` directive in `go.mod` pinning a
    patched version (e.g. `toolchain go1.26.3`) or bump the `go`
    directive to a known-good patch. This is **not** a US-0011 scope
    item — US-0011 is the wiring; the toolchain bump is a separate,
    isolated change.
  - `staticcheck` and `golangci-lint` were not run locally (not
    installed). CI will be the first place they execute. If either flags
    a finding in the existing codebase the immediate follow-up is to
    fix it; the lint config is intentionally strict because the security
    gate is part of the public contract.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **Patterns discovered**:
    - The CI gate has two natural tiers: the `test` job (correctness;
      runs on every push) and the `security` job (vulns + lint;
      independent so a slow vulnerability database fetch does not gate
      the test signal). `goreleaser-check` already lived in its own job
      for the same reason. Future gates (e.g. SBOM generation) should
      follow the same pattern.
    - Secret-leak tests are most effective when they exercise the
      *renderer* (Renderer.Error, Renderer.Logf, Renderer.Data) through
      the production exit-code path rather than constructing a
      Renderer in isolation. The integration suite catches missing
      wire-ups (e.g. a new command that bypassed `r.Logf`) that
      renderer-only unit tests would not.
    - GitHub Actions writes a workflow-injection warning whenever a
      run-step uses `${{ github.event.* }}` interpolation. Keep
      vulnerable inputs out of `run:` blocks — use `env:` indirection.
      None of the new workflow files use untrusted inputs in `run:`.
  - **Gotchas encountered**:
    - `actions/dependency-review-action@v4` only runs on `pull_request`
      events; pushing directly to a branch will not trigger it. That is
      intentional (the action diffs base-against-head) but worth
      documenting so contributors don't expect it from a push.
    - `golangci-lint` v2 requires a `version: 2` field in the config
      file; v1 does not accept it. The `.golangci.yml` here targets
      v1's grammar (no top-level `version:`); golangci-lint-action
      installs the latest stable, which currently still resolves to
      v1's compatible mode. If CI fails on schema, pin the action to a
      v1 release.
    - `gosec` flags G304 ("file inclusion via variable") on every
      `os.Open` taking a CLI-supplied path. That is the entire point of
      `--input <path>` and `--config <path>`; we explicitly exclude
      G304 in `.golangci.yml` and rely on path validation done in the
      command layer instead.
  - **Useful context**:
    - The redactor is defence-in-depth: classification of errors and
      explicit field handling are still primary. The new test file is
      a regression net, not the security boundary itself.
    - `verify.sh`'s "report, never silently skip" rule is a project
      principle, not a tooling quirk. The Ralph Loop iteration pattern
      depends on every gap being visible in `progress.txt` so future
      iterations can act on it.
---

## 2026-05-08 - US-0012
- **Implemented**: Curated command policy and naming conventions
  foundation. Locks the public contract that turns raw Dokploy OpenAPI
  operations into stable, agent-friendly yalla commands without
  sacrificing full coverage.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/curated/policy.go` (new) — `Domain` type + closed
    seven-element set (`project`, `app`, `compose`, `database`,
    `server`, `settings`, `provider`); `Domains()`/`IsDomain`;
    `PreferredVerbs` and `BannedVerbAliases` (with hint mappings to
    canonical replacements); kebab-case identifier regex; `Command`
    descriptor (`Path`, `Domain`, `Verb`, `Summary`, `OperationIDs`,
    `HumanExample`, `JSONExample`) and `Validate()` method that
    enforces every rule including the human/JSON example contract;
    `SortCommands` deterministic ordering helper.
  - `internal/curated/registry.go` (new) — `OperationLookup` interface
    (so tests do not need the full *api.Registry); `Registry` with
    `NewRegistry` (validates each command, rejects duplicate paths,
    sorts by Path), `Commands()` (defensive copy), `Len`, `ByDomain`,
    `VerifyAgainstSpec`; package-level `Default()` over the static
    `defaultCommands` slice (empty for now — future curated-command
    stories append).
  - `internal/curated/policy_test.go` (new) — covers happy path, every
    Validate failure mode (16 sub-cases), banned-alias / preferred-verb
    cross-check, and `SortCommands`.
  - `internal/curated/registry_test.go` (new) — covers empty
    construction, sorting, duplicate-path rejection, Validate
    propagation, defensive copy on `Commands()`, `ByDomain`,
    `VerifyAgainstSpec` happy/sad/nil paths, full nil-receiver safety,
    and `TestDefaultRegistry_VerifiesAgainstSpec` which is the live
    regression net against the embedded OpenAPI spec.
  - `internal/curated/AGENTS.md` (new) — module conventions for future
    contributors.
  - `internal/cli/manifest_cmd.go` — added `curated` import; extended
    `manifestDoc` with `CuratedDomains []string` and
    `CuratedCommands []manifestCuratedCommand`; added the
    `manifestCuratedCommand` JSON shape (`path`, `domain`, `verb`,
    `summary`, `operation_ids`, `human_example`, `json_example`); made
    `runManifest` accept a `*curated.Registry`; added
    `curatedDomainNames` and `collectCuratedCommands` projection
    helpers; updated the human-mode banner to surface curated counts.
  - `internal/cli/manifest_cmd_test.go` — new tests:
    `TestManifest_JSONListsCuratedDomains`,
    `TestManifest_JSONIncludesCuratedCommands` (checks the JSON wire
    shape directly so a missing field cannot be hidden by struct
    decoding), `TestManifest_RendersCuratedCommandPayload` (end-to-end
    projection with a custom registry, including the defensive-copy
    invariant), `TestManifest_DefaultCuratedRegistryMatchesAPISpec`
    (live spec regression mirror).
  - `internal/cli/AGENTS.md` — new bullet documenting the curated →
    manifest contract and the "never replace raw API coverage" rule.
  - `docs/curated-commands.md` (new) — the human-facing policy
    document. Covers domains, naming rules, recommended verb sets per
    domain, the manifest contract (with sample JSON), and the
    step-by-step add-a-curated-command workflow.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — set US-0012 `passes: true`.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass (curated package included).
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages pass.
  - `bash scripts/verify.sh --quiet` — full local gate clean; optional
    tools (`govulncheck`, `staticcheck`, `golangci-lint`, `goreleaser`)
    reported as not installed exactly as the project policy requires.
  - Smoke: `go run ./cmd/yalla --json manifest | jq '.data |
    {curated_domains, curated_commands}'` returns the canonical
    seven-domain list and an empty `curated_commands` array.
- **Tools NOT available locally** (per project policy): `goimports`,
  `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`. Same
  status as previous iterations; documented here so the gap stays
  visible.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The `defaultCommands` slice is empty by design — every subsequent
    curated-command story (US-001x onward) MUST append its descriptor
    there, with a non-empty `OperationIDs` list, before wiring the
    Cobra command. The manifest, docs, and validation plumbing pick
    them up automatically.
  - The Cobra-command Examples for any curated command should mirror
    `Command.HumanExample` / `Command.JSONExample` so the manifest,
    `--help`, and `docs/curated-commands.md` stay in sync. A future
    helper that returns a Cobra-style `Example` block from a
    `curated.Command` would centralise that, but is out of scope
    for the foundation story.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Foundation stories pay off when they bake in the contract test:
    `TestDefaultRegistry_VerifiesAgainstSpec` (and its manifest mirror)
    means a stale operationId fails CI before the binary ships.
    Without it, curated commands could drift from the OpenAPI spec
    silently.
  - Decoding the manifest into a `map[string]json.RawMessage` (rather
    than the typed struct) is the cleanest way to assert "this JSON
    field exists on the wire". The struct decoder cheerfully
    zero-fills missing fields, hiding regressions.
  - Defensive-copy assertions belong on every projection that returns
    a slice ("mutate the projected slice, re-read from the source,
    expect no bleed"). Catching a shared backing array in test is much
    cheaper than chasing the eventual consistency bug.
  - Banning short aliases via a hint-bearing map (`{"ls": "list"}`)
    gives validation errors that tell the developer the right answer
    instead of just rejecting the wrong one. Worth replicating any
    time the policy is "thou shalt not X" and there is a canonical Y.
  - The `OperationLookup` interface kept the curated tests free of the
    OpenAPI embed dance. The same pattern (a tiny adapter interface)
    would help any future cross-package contract test.
---

## 2026-05-08 - API-0001
- **Implemented**: First per-operation API coverage story
  (admin-setupMonitoring, POST /admin.setupMonitoring, tag admin) plus
  the scalable, append-only contract-test harness every subsequent
  API-XXXX story will reuse.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` (new) — defines
    `apiCoverageCase` (the per-story input record) and
    `coveredAPIOperations` (the append-only slice). Five
    table-driven tests run the same assertions per case:
    `TestAPICoverage_RegistryInvariants` (id/method/path/tag match
    `api.Default()`), `TestAPICoverage_SchemaCommandWorks`
    (`yalla schema get --json` envelope), `TestAPICoverage_ManifestListsOperation`
    (operation id is in `yalla manifest --json` operations.ids),
    `TestAPICoverage_SuccessRoundTrip` (httptest server validates
    method, path, Authorization header, content-type, body, and the
    success envelope shape), and `TestAPICoverage_RepresentativeFailure`
    (default 401 → CodeAuth, configurable per case). Fixture body for
    admin-setupMonitoring mirrors the openapi.json schema's required
    shape (`metricsConfig` with `server.refreshRate` + `containers`).
  - `internal/cli/AGENTS.md` — new "Per-operation API coverage"
    section documenting the harness contract for future API-XXXX
    contributors (sorted-by-story-id, override FailureStatus/Code
    when 401 is not the right representative failure, SampleBody
    must mirror the OpenAPI required shape verbatim).
  - `ralph/prd.json` — flipped `API-0001.passes` to `true`.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean (one auto-format pass on the new test file).
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — no changes.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass; the five new
    `TestAPICoverage_*` subtests under `API-0001/admin-setupMonitoring`
    PASS.
  - `go test -race ./...` — clean.
  - `bash scripts/verify.sh --quiet` — full local gate clean. The
    same four optional tools as in earlier stories
    (`govulncheck`, `staticcheck`, `golangci-lint`, `goreleaser`)
    are still not installed locally and are documented in the
    verify.sh banner; this matches the project's "report, never
    silently skip" policy.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The harness defaults the failure leg to 401→CodeAuth. A handful
    of upcoming operations (rate-limit-prone create/delete, conflict-
    prone update) will want to override `FailureStatus` /
    `FailureCode` to assert the more representative path; document
    that as a checklist item when we get to those PRD entries.
  - Body fixtures are currently free-form JSON. We do **not**
    validate the fixture against the OpenAPI request schema today;
    if a fixture drifts from the schema, only the wire-side equality
    check protects us. A future enhancement (post-coverage) is to
    pipe each `SampleBody` through a schema validator using the
    embedded JSON schema fragments — out of scope for this story.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **One harness for ~440 stories**: hand-rolling a separate test
    file per API-XXXX story would mean ~440 near-identical files and
    the same number of code-review rituals. The data-driven slice
    keeps the per-story diff to a single struct literal while still
    giving each story a named subtest path
    (`TestAPICoverage_*/API-XXXX/<operationId>`) so a regression
    points back to its PRD entry without grep gymnastics.
  - **Use `setupAPICallServer` from the existing test suite**: the
    helper wires an httptest server to `YALLA_BASE_URL` and seeds
    `YALLA_TOKEN` via `t.Setenv`, which means our coverage tests
    cannot run with `t.Parallel()` — same constraint as every other
    env-mutating test in `internal/cli`.
  - **Body equality through JSON normalize**: comparing wire bytes
    directly fails on whitespace differences (the CLI re-serialises
    JSON through json.Decoder which collapses pretty-printed
    fixtures). Round-tripping both sides through `json.Unmarshal` +
    `json.Marshal` before comparison eliminates the flake without
    losing structural fidelity.
  - **Per-operation registry assertion is intentionally redundant**:
    `internal/api/registry_test.go` already locks the full operation
    set; per-story checks duplicate that for a precise reason — when
    an assertion fails, the failing subtest name names the PRD entry
    so the contributor knows which story to revisit instead of
    decoding which row in a 446-element slice broke.
---

    to `coveredAPIOperations`, sorted-by-StoryID. SampleBody mirrors
    the OpenAPI required shape verbatim (`applicationId` string only).
    SuccessResponse is `{}` because the spec defines the 200 body as
    an empty object with `additionalProperties: false`; the harness
    asserts envelope `data.method` / `data.status` rather than any
    payload projection, so an empty success body is the correct
    fixture.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — flipped `API-0011.passes` to `true`.

  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./internal/cli/ -run TestAPICoverage -count=1` — all
    five `TestAPICoverage_*/API-0011/application-cancelDeployment`
    subtests pass.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass.
  - `go test -race ./...` — clean.
  - `bash scripts/verify.sh --quiet` — full local gate clean.
- **Tools NOT available locally** (per project policy, reported by
  verify.sh, not silently skipped): `goimports`, `govulncheck`,
  `staticcheck`, `golangci-lint`, `goreleaser`. Same status as every
  prior iteration.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The default failure leg (401 → CodeAuth) is the right
    representative path for `application-cancelDeployment`: the spec
    declares 400/401/403/500 and 401 is the canonical authenticated
    failure. No FailureStatus / FailureCode override needed.
  - Body fixture is hand-rolled JSON; the future schema-validator
    enhancement noted in API-0001's progress entry would have caught
    a typo automatically, but for a single-string-field schema the
    risk is negligible.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **The harness scales as designed**: a brand-new API story now
    costs one struct literal (16 lines including comment) plus a PRD
    flip. The ~440 remaining API stories follow the exact same diff
    shape — keep coveredAPIOperations sorted by StoryID and the
    diffs read as contiguous inserts.
  - **Empty-object success responses are valid fixtures**: when the
    OpenAPI 200 body is `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`, set
    `SuccessResponse: "{}"`. The harness round-trips both sides
    through json.Marshal before comparing, so an empty body still
    produces a stable success envelope (`data.status: 200`,
    `data.method: "POST"`).
  - **Single-required-field operations need no special handling**:
    the SampleBody must include the required field with a plausible
    value; everything else (path-param plumbing, query plumbing) the
    harness ignores when the corresponding map is empty.

  `yalla api call` executor.

  (`name`, `apiUrl`, `apiKey`, `model`, `isEnabled`), so the fixture
  supplies all five with deterministic-but-clearly-fake values. The
  bytes only ever live in a per-test `t.TempDir()` so the placeholder
  `apiKey` never escapes the test process.

  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` (+22 lines, sorted-by-StoryID insert
    between API-0001 and API-0011).

  - `ralph/progress.txt` (this entry).

  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages PASS.
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages PASS.
  - `bash scripts/verify.sh --quiet` — `all checks passed`.
  - Optional tools (`govulncheck`, `staticcheck`, `golangci-lint`,
    `goreleaser`) remain not installed locally; `verify.sh` documents the
    skip list explicitly per CONTRIBUTING.md (not silently skipped).
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups:** None for this story. The 200 response
  is `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`, identical to the
  application-* peers, so the success-leg envelope assertion stays focused
  on `data.method` / `data.status` rather than payload projection. Future
  iterations covering richer `ai.*` operations may want to add a non-401
  representative failure (e.g. 422 on schema rejection) once an `ai-update`
  / `ai-list` story lands.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - For an API-XXXX story whose OpenAPI request body has only required
    primitives (string/bool), the cheapest correct fixture is to enumerate
    every required field with deterministic placeholders. The harness
    round-trips bytes through `json.Unmarshal`, so any whitespace style is
    fine, but field *names* must match the spec verbatim.
  - The `ai` tag is the third tag covered (after `admin`, `application`).
    `internal/api/registry_test.go` already lists `"ai"` in the expected
    tag set, so no test-side change was needed; future stories adding a
    *new* tag will need to update that slice and `TestAPICoverage_*` will
    fail on `op.Tag != tc.Tag` if the registry does not surface it.
  - The append-only `coveredAPIOperations` slice keeps story diffs down
    to one contiguous insert. Sticking to the StoryID sort order means
    `git blame` on a single line points back to the right commit even
    when later stories add unrelated entries above or below.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0014

- **Story:** Cover application-create (POST /application.create), tag
  `application`, priority 2.
- **What shipped:** Appended one `apiCoverageCase{...}` literal to
  `coveredAPIOperations` in `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` so the
  existing five-part harness (registry invariants, `yalla schema get`
  envelope, `yalla manifest` inclusion, httptest-driven success
  round-trip, default 401 → `CodeAuth` representative failure leg) now
  also gates `application-create`. No production-code changes — the
  operation is already wired through the embedded OpenAPI spec
  (`internal/api/data/openapi.json`), the singleton `api.Default()`
  registry, and the raw `yalla api call` executor.
- **Sample body:** Unlike prior delete/cancel-shaped peers, the spec
  marks two top-level fields as required (`name`, `environmentId`) and
  three optional fields (`appName`, plus `description` and `serverId`
  declared as `anyOf [string, null]`). The fixture supplies all five
  with the string branch of the `anyOf` so the wire payload stays valid
  against either schema branch once a future schema validator is wired
  into the harness. Per-case fixture token (`*-cov-app-create-0014`)
  remains unique so a `git grep` traces back to the exact PRD story.
- **Files changed:**
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` (+27 lines, sorted-by-StoryID
    insert between API-0013 and the closing `}` of `coveredAPIOperations`).
  - `ralph/prd.json` (`API-0014.passes` flipped `false` → `true`).
  - `ralph/progress.txt` (this entry).
- **Verification:**
  - `gofmt -l .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./internal/cli/ -run 'TestAPICoverage' -count=1` — all
    `TestAPICoverage_*/API-0014/application-create` subtests PASS
    alongside API-0001…API-0013.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages PASS.
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages PASS.
  - `bash scripts/verify.sh --quiet` — `all checks passed`. Optional
    tools (`govulncheck`, `staticcheck`, `golangci-lint`, `goreleaser`)
    remain not installed locally and are documented in the verify.sh
    skip banner per CONTRIBUTING.md (not silently skipped).
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups:**
  - Default 401 → `CodeAuth` representative failure leg is appropriate;
    the spec also declares 400 (BAD_REQUEST), 403 (FORBIDDEN), and 500
    (INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR), but those broader status-mapping invariants
    are already exercised cross-cuttingly by `internal/api/client_test.go`.
  - We do not yet validate `SampleBody` against the OpenAPI request
    schema. With API-0014 introducing the first `anyOf` field in the
    coverage suite, drift between the fixture and the `[string, null]`
    branches would only be caught by the wire-side equality assertion
    today. Wiring a schema validator into the harness becomes more
    valuable as create/update operations land.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **Two-required-field create operations are still a single-line cost.**
    API-0014 added 27 lines with no harness changes despite being the
    first covered case with multiple required fields. Future
    `*-create` / `*-update` operations should expect the same
    one-struct-literal pattern even when the body grows wider.
  - **`anyOf [string, null]` is a recurring Dokploy idiom.** Treat the
    string branch as the canonical fixture variant: it satisfies both
    schema branches and exercises real serialisation, whereas a `null`
    fixture would skip the body-forwarding round-trip on the field. As
    more `*-create` / `*-update` operations land (API-0015 onward), the
    same convention keeps fixtures consistent and grep-friendly.
  - **Application-namespace fixture tokens should encode the verb.**
    The slug pattern `app-cov-<verb>-<seq>` (`app-cov-cancel-deploy-0011`,
    `app-cov-clean-queues-0012`, `app-cov-clear-deploys-0013`,
    `*-cov-app-create-0014`) keeps a single grep pointing back to the
    PRD story even when the operationId namespace gets crowded.

  the raw `yalla api call` executor.

  covered so far — six required strings (`environmentId`, `id`,
  `dockerCompose`, `envVariables`, `name`, `description`) plus three
  optionals (`serverId`, `domains[]`, `configFiles[]`). Per the
  API-0014 convention we supply every optional with deterministic-but-
  clearly-fake values so the wire-payload assertion exercises the full
  deploy envelope, including the array branches with their own nested
  required fields (`domains[].host/port/serviceName` and
  `configFiles[].filePath/content`). The per-case fixture token
  `*-cov-ai-deploy-0004` keeps `git grep` traceable to this PRD story.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended one
    `apiCoverageCase` entry between API-0003 and API-0011 (sorted by
    StoryID).

  - `ralph/progress.txt` — this entry.
- **Verification run**: `gofmt -w .`, `go mod tidy`, `go vet ./...`,
  `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, plus a focused
  `go test ./internal/cli -run 'TestAPICoverage' -v -count=1`. All
  green; the new `API-0004/ai-deploy` subtests in both
  `TestAPICoverage_SuccessRoundTrip` and
  `TestAPICoverage_RepresentativeFailure` pass on the first run.
- **Tools not installed locally** (documented per the Codebase Patterns
  baseline rather than silently skipped): `goimports`, `golangci-lint`,
  `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`. CI still runs the
  security/lint matrix on push.
- **Results**: All required gates pass. `ai-deploy` is now
  registry-checked, schema-reachable, manifest-listed, success-round-
  tripped through `httptest.Server`, and failure-mapped to `CodeAuth`
  on 401.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**: None for this story. The next
  pending API story by priority is API-0005 (`ai-get`, GET /ai.get),
  still `passes: false`. ai-get is a GET-no-body operation; coverage
  there will exercise the parameter-less GET branch of the harness
  (no SampleBody, no Content-Type forwarding).
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **Multi-line string literals (e.g. `dockerCompose` YAML,
    `envVariables` `KEY=VAL` pairs) survive the `json.RawMessage` →
    `json.Unmarshal` → `json.Marshal` round-trip in
    `runAPICoverageSuccess` cleanly. No special escaping needed beyond
    standard JSON quoting.**
  - **Array-of-object request fields with their own `required` blocks
    (here `domains[]` and `configFiles[]`) cost zero harness changes:
    the canonical body-equality assertion already normalises through
    `encoding/json`, so nested arrays are diff-stable without a
    sort-stable comparator.**
  - **`/ai.deploy` is the first ai/* operation to mix required strings
    with optional arrays. The same shape repeats for many curated
    deploy endpoints (compose-deploy, application-deploy variants);
    keeping the `*-cov-<short>-<storyId>` naming convention on every
    fixture string makes spec-drift bisection a single `git grep`.**

## 2026-05-08 - API-0021 (application-one, GET /application.one)
- Added the per-operation contract entry for `application-one` to
  `coveredAPIOperations` in `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`. This is
  the **first** GET-shaped entry in the application/* coverage roster:
  no request body, a single required query parameter `applicationId`
  (string), expressed via `SampleQuery: map[string][]string{"applicationId":
  {"app-cov-one-0021"}}`. The shared harness (success/failure/registry/
  schema/manifest sub-tests) picks the entry up automatically.
- Files changed:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` (+24 lines, one struct literal).
  - `ralph/prd.json` (`API-0021.passes` → `true`).
  - `ralph/progress.txt` (this entry).
- Verification run (from repo root):
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — no diff.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — PASS, including the new
    `TestAPICoverage_*/API-0021/application-one` sub-tests across registry,
    schema, manifest, success-leg, and failure-leg checks.
  - `go test -race ./...` — PASS.
  - `goimports` not installed locally — skipped (documented in earlier
    progress entries; no new imports introduced by this change).
- Remaining risks / follow-ups: none specific to this story. The `coveredAPIOperations`
  list now exercises the `SampleQuery` branch of `runAPICoverageSuccess`
  end-to-end for the first time, so any future regression in query-string
  forwarding will surface here as well as in the dedicated CLI tests.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The existing harness already supports GET operations via `SampleQuery`
    and `SamplePathParams` — no harness change is needed when adding a
    GET-with-query coverage story. `runAPICoverageSuccess` re-reads
    `r.URL.Query()` and the comparator at line ~644 enforces verbatim
    propagation of every key the case declares.
  - `buildCoverageInputArgs` writes a `query` field (and/or
    `path_params` / `body`) into a tempfile and returns `--input <path>`
    so the CLI exercises the same `--input` JSON shape end-to-end. New
    GET stories should populate `SampleQuery` rather than synthesising
    URL strings.
  - Operations whose 200 response is the spec's `{}` empty-object can
    safely keep `SuccessResponse: \`{}\`` — the success-leg envelope
    assertion focuses on `data.method` / `data.status`, not payload
    projection, matching every prior application/* peer.

    accepts our placeholders, but a future schema validator will too,
    so don't be tempted to omit the "irrelevant" mode-specific fields.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0030 - Cover application-saveEnvironment (POST /application.saveEnvironment)
- Implemented per-operation contract coverage for
  `application-saveEnvironment` by appending a single
  `apiCoverageCase` literal (StoryID `API-0030`) to
  `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`'s `coveredAPIOperations`
  slice. The append-only harness automatically asserts all five
  invariants: registry id/method/path/tag, `yalla schema get`,
  `yalla manifest`, httptest success round-trip with body
  forwarding, and a representative 401 → `E_AUTH` failure path.
- Unlike API-0027 / API-0029 (all-required-nullable-string bodies)
  and API-0028 (mixed required strings + closed enum), the
  saveEnvironment body is a NEW shape: 1 plain required string
  (`applicationId`), 3 required nullable strings (`env`,
  `buildArgs`, `buildSecrets`), and 1 required boolean
  (`createEnvFile`). Every required field is populated with a
  distinct `app-cov-env-<slug>-0030` placeholder; the boolean is
  set to `true` so the success-leg wire payload stays as close to
  a realistic happy-path call as the harness allows.
- Files changed:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended the API-0030
    case after API-0029 (one contiguous block; no other touches).
  - `ralph/prd.json` — flipped `passes: false` → `passes: true`
    for `API-0030`.
  - `ralph/progress.txt` — this entry.
- Verification run from repo root:
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — no module changes.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass; `internal/cli` 1.163s.
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages pass; `internal/cli` 2.417s.
  - `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`,
    `goreleaser` — not installed locally, intentionally skipped per
    Codebase Patterns guidance.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none for this story. The next
  application/* coverage gaps are API-0031 through API-0034 (the
  remaining `save*Provider` family — gitea/github/gitlab/git). Spot-
  checking the spec suggests they share API-0027/0029's
  many-required-nullable-string shape; the API-0029 fixture skeleton
  should transfer with only operationId and field-name swaps.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - `application-saveEnvironment` is the FIRST application/*
    operation whose required body mixes plain-string +
    nullable-string + boolean shapes in one payload. Future
    application/* stories with similar mixed-shape bodies should
    pattern-match against API-0030's literal rather than against
    API-0027 / API-0029 (whose bodies are uniform-nullable-string)
    or API-0028 (whose `buildType` is a closed enum, not a
    boolean). The contiguous, story-id-sorted ordering of
    `coveredAPIOperations` makes this pattern-match a quick read.
  - Booleans in required body fields don't need a placeholder
    convention — pick the value that keeps the fixture closest to
    a realistic happy-path call (here: `createEnvFile: true`).
    Validators care about presence + type, and the wire payload
    asserts only that the body round-trips intact.
  - The `app-cov-<tag>-<slug>-<storyID>` placeholder convention
    continues to pay off: distinct values per field make any future
    schema-validator failure message unambiguously point at the
    offending field, and the `app-cov-*` prefix doubles as a
    grep-friendly marker that reviewers can use to recognise body-
    field secrets (`buildSecrets` here, `password` in API-0029) as
    fixture data rather than real credentials.

  - `application-saveGiteaProvider` is the canonical literal for
    the `save*Provider` family pattern: one plain-string
    `applicationId` + N nullable-string vendor fields. API-0032
    (github) should pattern-match against this entry and add only
    the closed-enum `triggerType` placeholder. The contiguous,
    story-id-sorted ordering of `coveredAPIOperations` keeps the
    family readable as one block.
  - Optional schema fields (here: `enableSubmodules`,
    `watchPaths`) stay out of the SampleBody when the harness
    only exercises the happy-path required surface — the success-
    leg envelope assertion focuses on `data.method` /
    `data.status` and the wire payload only needs to round-trip
    intact. This keeps fixtures minimal-but-valid and matches every
    prior application/* peer.
  - The `app-cov-<vendor>-<slug>-<storyID>` placeholder convention
    continues to scale: `app-cov-gitea-*` here parallels
    `app-cov-docker-*` (API-0029) and `app-cov-env-*` (API-0030),
    so a grep for `app-cov-` cleanly indexes every fixture-as-
    test-data marker across the suite.

  extends the gitea/github shape with two nullable fields —
  `gitlabProjectId` (`anyOf:[number,null]`, the FIRST numeric-typed
  required field across application/* coverage) and
  `gitlabPathNamespace` (`anyOf:[string,null]`). API-0034
  (custom git) is the outlier of the family: its required set
  includes `watchPaths` (`anyOf:[array<string>,null]`) rather
  than an `*Id` field, so its fixture must populate `watchPaths`

  inside an array) rather than omitting it.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - `application-saveGithubProvider` is now the canonical literal
    for `save*Provider` family entries that extend the gitea
    shape with vendor-specific REQUIRED fields. API-0033 (gitlab)
    should pattern-match against this entry — copy the
    seven-field body, swap the `github*` slugs to `gitlab*`, and
    add the two extra fields. Importantly, `gitlabProjectId` is
    `anyOf:[number,null]`, so its placeholder MUST be a numeric
    JSON literal (e.g. `1032`, mirroring the trailing story-id
    digits convention) rather than the usual `app-cov-*` string —
    this will be the first application/* fixture with a non-
    string required value.
  - Closed-enum required fields (`triggerType` here) should
    always be populated with the schema's `default` value when
    the enum has a default. This keeps the fixture valid against
    both the current happy-path harness AND any future
    enum-aware validator without forcing the test author to
    audit the enum at fixture-write time.
  - The contiguous, story-id-sorted ordering of
    `coveredAPIOperations` continues to scale: API-0030 → 0031
    → 0032 are now one readable block of three application/*
    `save*Provider` entries with progressively richer required
    surfaces (env-vars boolean, gitea nullable strings, github
    closed enum). Reviewers can scan the block top-down to see
    the family pattern emerge.
  - The `app-cov-<vendor>-<slug>-<storyID>` placeholder
    convention scales cleanly: `app-cov-github-*` here parallels
    `app-cov-gitea-*` (API-0031), `app-cov-env-*` (API-0030), and
    `app-cov-docker-*` (API-0029), so a grep for `app-cov-`
    cleanly indexes every fixture-as-test-data marker across the
    suite without false positives from real credentials.

    per-family.

    contiguous five-entry block of application/save*Provider coverage
    that future readers can pattern-match in a single screenful.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0035
- **Implemented**: Coverage entry for the GET `/application.search` Dokploy
  operation (`application-search`). Appends a single `apiCoverageCase` to
  `coveredAPIOperations` so the existing harness automatically proves
  registry presence, schema reachability, manifest inclusion, an httptest-
  backed success leg with verbatim query-param round-trip, and a 401 →
  `E_AUTH` failure leg — all without new helper code.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — append API-0035 entry. Method
    `GET`, path `/application.search`, tag `application`, no body.
    `SampleQuery` populates a representative subset (`q`, `owner`,
    `limit`, `offset`) of the spec's 11 all-optional parameters; numeric
    values (`limit`/`offset`) travel as decimal strings to match the
    untyped wire grammar of HTTP query strings. `SuccessResponse: "{}"`
    mirrors the spec's `additionalProperties:false` empty 200 body.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — set API-0035 `passes: true`.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — `ok github.com/JuribaDev/yalla/internal/cli` (full
    coverage harness incl. registry/schema/manifest/success/failure
    legs).
  - `go test -race ./...` — clean.
- **Tools NOT available locally** (per the verification policy):
  `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The fixture only exercises 4 of 11 query parameters. That is
    intentional (representative coverage of free-text + scoped filter +
    numeric pagination) but a future schema-validator wiring may want
    to extend the case to all 11 to lock the full param surface.
  - `application-search` is the FIRST application/* coverage entry with
    fully-optional query params. If a future Dokploy revision tightens
    `required: true` on any of these fields, the registry-invariant
    test will still pass but agents would silently get 4xx — only the
    spec digest pinning (`sourceApi.sha256` in `ralph/prd.json` and
    `EmbeddedSpecSHA256`) catches that drift.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - All-optional query parameters are wire-valid empty: the harness
    handles a fully-empty `SampleQuery` (see API-0006 `ai-getAll`) but
    when params *do* exist the verbatim-comparator at
    `runAPICoverageSuccess` insists they survive the
    `--input.query` → request-URL round-trip exactly, including
    multi-valued slices and ordering.
  - Numeric-typed query params (`limit`/`offset`, both spec-typed as
    `number`) are sent over the wire as their decimal-string form
    inside `--input`'s `query: map[string][]string`. There is no
    separate "numeric" query input shape; HTTP query strings are
    untyped.
  - When extending the coverage table with a new entry, prefer to
    populate a *representative* subset of optional fields (free-text +
    scoped + numeric) rather than the full surface. The
    success-leg comparator is precise about whatever you put in; the
    bigger the fixture, the larger the diff blast-radius if Dokploy
    renames a param. Subset coverage hits the contract bits that
    matter (verbatim CLI propagation + numeric grammar) without
    over-specifying.

    cache; the race build is the one that takes the bulk of the wall time
    (~2.5s for `internal/cli`). Don't worry about cache-bust flags for
    coverage-only stories — `-count=1` is sufficient if you suspect cache
    skew, but plain `go test ./...` is enough when you've just edited a

## 2026-05-08 - API-0038
- **Implemented**: Per-operation contract coverage for Dokploy `application-update`
  (POST /application.update, tag `application`). Single new
  `apiCoverageCase` literal in `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`. The
  shared harness derives registry/method/path/tag invariants, schema-get
  envelope, manifest inclusion, an httptest-backed success exchange, and
  a 401→`E_AUTH` failure path from that one entry — no production-code
  change is required because `application-update` was already wired into
  the embedded OpenAPI registry and surfaced through `yalla api call` /
  `yalla schema get` / `yalla manifest --json` from US-0004 onward.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — new struct literal for
    `API-0038`, sorted after `API-0037` (application-stop).
  - `ralph/prd.json` — set US-API-0038 `passes: true`.
- **Schema notes**: request body schema lists `applicationId` as the
  only required field; every other property (name, appName, env,
  buildType, swarm config blobs, per-git-provider IDs, resource limits,
  preview flags, …) is optional with anyOf-null semantics. The
  minimal-but-valid fixture is therefore `{"applicationId": "..."}`,
  staying robust if a future schema validator is wired into the harness.
  200 response is `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`, matching
  every other application/* mutation peer.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages `ok`; `internal/cli` runtime 1.137s.
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages `ok`; `internal/cli` runtime
    2.431s with the race detector on.
- **Tools NOT available locally** (unchanged from prior stories):
  `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`,
  `goreleaser`.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**: none. The harness covers registry
  invariants, schema reachability, manifest inclusion, success
  round-trip, and the representative failure path from the same single
  entry.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The Dokploy `application-update` schema is the largest in the
    `application` tag (90+ optional properties spanning app metadata,
    swarm config, every supported git provider, build args, registry
    IDs, preview deployment toggles, and resource limits). The required
    set is still just `applicationId`, so the minimal-applicationId
    POST family fixture (used by start/stop/reload/redeploy/delete) is
    the right baseline — adding optional fields would only matter once
    the harness gains a JSON-Schema validator. Until then, less is
    more.
  - When a schema's `additionalProperties` is *unset* (vs `false`),
    JSON Schema treats it as permissive. `application-update` request
    body falls into that bucket, so a future validator would not
    reject extra keys; do not over-tighten the fixture for that
    reason alone.
---

## 2026-05-08 - API-0039
- **Implemented**: Coverage of Dokploy API operation
  `application-updateTraefikConfig` (POST `/application.updateTraefikConfig`)
  via the shared `apiCoverageCase` harness.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended a single
    `apiCoverageCase` for API-0039. Unlike the broader minimal-applicationId
    POST family, the OpenAPI schema requires *both* `applicationId` and
    `traefikConfig` strings (no optional siblings), so the minimal-but-valid
    fixture carries both. `traefikConfig` is a literal YAML snippet (with
    embedded newlines + colon) so the success leg exercises real JSON
    serialisation of an embedded raw-text payload through `yalla api call`.
    Success response is `{}` per spec, matching every other application/*
    mutation peer.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — set API-0039 `passes: true`.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -l .` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages `ok`.
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages `ok`.
- **Tools NOT available locally** (unchanged): `goimports`, `golangci-lint`,
  `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The fixture's `traefikConfig` value is syntactically YAML but the
    Dokploy server is not exercised against a real Traefik validator in
    the test (httptest server returns `{}` for any request). A future
    contract-test layer that round-trips a known-bad Traefik string
    against a stricter mock would catch validation regressions; out of
    scope for raw API coverage stories.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - When a request schema requires multiple top-level strings (not just
    `applicationId`), keep the fixture minimal-but-valid by including
    every required field. The harness forwards bytes verbatim, so an
    incomplete body would still pass the success leg today (the httptest
    server doesn't validate) but would mask schema drift if Dokploy ever
    tightened its server-side validator.
  - Embedding newlines and colons inside a JSON string field is a useful
    smoke test for the encoder path — the harness sends bytes verbatim,
    but a future validator hook would reject improperly escaped values.
---

- Appended a single `apiCoverageCase` entry for `compose-create` to
  `coveredAPIOperations` in `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`. This is
  the fourth entry in the `compose/*` roster.
- Unlike the prior three compose entries (cancelDeployment / cleanQueues /
  clearDeployments), `compose-create` is NOT in the minimal-id-only POST
  family. Its required body fields are `name` and `environmentId` per
  `internal/api/data/openapi.json`, with optional `appName`, `composeFile`,
  `composeType` (enum docker-compose|stack), `description` (anyOf
  [string,null]), and `serverId` (anyOf [string,null]).
- Direct precedent: API-0014 (application-create) — same "create" shape
  with same anyOf [string,null] handling. Fixture sends the string variant
  for description/serverId and exercises the full payload (all 7 fields)
  so a future schema validator wired into the harness still accepts it.
- 200 response per spec is `{}` (additionalProperties: false), matching
  the empty-success convention shared by every prior application/* and
  compose/* mutation peer; SuccessResponse stays `{}`.
- The shared harness derives registry / schema get / manifest inclusion /
  httptest success round-trip / 401 -> E_AUTH failure assertions from
  the entry alone, so no production-code change was required.
- Files changed: `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`, `ralph/prd.json`,
  `ralph/progress.txt`.
- Verification: `gofmt -w .`, `go mod tidy`, `go vet ./...`,
  `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...` — all pass.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups: none specific to this story. Future
  compose/* entries (API-0070+) will continue the roster; many will
  also follow the minimal-composeId POST shape, but a few (delete,
  deploy, redeploy, refreshToken, etc.) will need their own per-spec
  fixtures the way create does.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The `coveredAPIOperations` registry handles the entire compose-create
    coverage with one struct literal — no production code changes
    needed. The shared harness in `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`
    is the single point of contract enforcement.
  - For "create" operations across tags, look first to the
    application-create precedent (API-0014). The compose-create payload
    is structurally identical except for the `composeType` enum and the
    `composeFile` string, both of which are optional.
  - When a field uses `anyOf: [string, null]`, send the string variant
    in the fixture so it stays valid against either branch once a
    schema validator is added.
  - Use deterministic-but-clearly-fake values (`yalla-coverage-*`,
    `env-cov-*`, `srv-cov-*`) so the fixture is greppable to its
    story ID and never collides with another case.

  - go test -race ./... — all packages pass under the race detector
    (internal/cli 2.494s).
  - goimports / golangci-lint / staticcheck / govulncheck / goreleaser
    not installed locally; documented as missing per project convention.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - 399 stories still report passes:false. Next compose/* story is
    API-0078 (compose-import, POST). The compose/* GET roster is now
    three entries strong (API-0075/0076/0077); subsequent compose/*
    POSTs follow API-0066-style minimal-composeId fixtures unless the
    spec dictates otherwise.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - DO NOT assume sibling spec parity from the title alone.
    `compose-getTags` reads superficially like API-0075/0076 (single
    query param, GET, 200=`{}`), but its lone `baseUrl` query param is
    declared OPTIONAL in `data/openapi.json` (no `required: true`),
    whereas API-0075/0076 carry a REQUIRED `composeId`. The
    `coveredAPIOperations` harness does not gate on `required`-ness —
    it simply forwards `SampleQuery` and re-reads `r.URL.Query()` —
    so the entry shape stays identical, but the comment block and
    SampleQuery key/value must reflect the actual spec (here
    `baseUrl: https://...` rather than another `composeId` UUID).
    Always re-confirm via `paths['/compose.<verb>'].get.parameters`
    before copy-pasting.
  - When the natural data type for an optional query parameter is a
    URL (as with `baseUrl`), use a `https://compose-cov-<slug>-<storyID>
    .example` literal so the slug stays greppable while still being a
    syntactically valid URL — test fixtures should still type-check
    against any future request-validator that lands on the optional
    path.
  - The `compose-cov-<verb>-<storyID>` slug convention scales to
    multi-word verbs by kebab-casing (`get-tags`, `get-default-command`,
    `get-converted-compose`, `clean-queues`, `clear-deployments`,
    `cancel-deployment`). New compose/* entries should keep this
    convention so cross-story grep continues to find every fixture.

    query param with an OPTIONAL enum-restricted one. The compose/* GET
    roster now has two structural sub-shapes:
      * single REQUIRED query string: API-0075/0076/0077.
      * two REQUIRED query strings: API-0081 (loadMountsByService).
      * one REQUIRED + one OPTIONAL enum query: API-0082 (loadServices) — new.

    the NON-DEFAULT enum value. This makes the wire-level transmission
    distinguishable from the server-side default-application behavior,
    so a future schema validator (or even a `--verbose` log diff) can
    confirm the CLI is forwarding the value verbatim. Default values
    are a wire-invisibility hazard worth avoiding in fixtures.

    shape templates: minimal-composeId-only POST (API-0066/0067/0068/0073),
    full create envelope (API-0069), composeId+deleteVolumes
    (API-0070), composeId+optional-title/description POST (API-0071),
    template envelope (API-0072), single-required-query GET
    (API-0075/0076/0077), POST with two required body fields
    (API-0078/0080), POST with required+optional split
    (API-0079), POST with required composeId+suffix (API-0080),
    multi-required-query GET (API-0081), and required+optional-enum
    GET (API-0082). Future compose/* stories should pattern-match
    against these rather than re-deriving from the spec.

  stories remain with passes=false out of 462. Next priority-2 compose/*
  candidate is API-0084 (compose-one, GET) per `(priority, id)` sort
  order.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - compose-move is the second POST-with-two-required-body-fields entry
    in the compose/* roster (after API-0078 compose-import). The compose/*
    POST sub-shapes now codified in the harness:
      * minimal-composeId-only POST (API-0066/0067/0068/0073/0074/0080) —
        single required `composeId` field, six members.
      * required+optional split POST (API-0079) — required `composeId`
        plus optional siblings.
      * two-required-fields POST (API-0078, 0083) — two non-overlapping
        required string fields. API-0078 carries a base64 payload field;
        API-0083 carries a target-environment FK. Future compose/* POST
        stories that match this 2-required shape (e.g. potential
        environment/project moves) should pattern-match against API-0083.
  - The compose/* coverage roster has now been extended to:
    GET trio (API-0075/0076/0077), single-required-query GET
    (API-0075/0076/0077), POST with two required body fields
    (API-0078, 0083), POST with required+optional split (API-0079),
    minimal-composeId-only POST (API-0066/0067/0068/0073/0074/0080),
    multi-required-query GET (API-0081), and required+optional-enum GET
    (API-0082). Future compose/* stories should pattern-match against
    these rather than re-deriving from the spec.
  - When the second required field is a foreign key (FK) to another
    domain entity (e.g. `targetEnvironmentId` -> environments), use a
    distinct slug prefix in the fixture so cross-story grep can still
    distinguish "the compose record being acted on" from "the related
    entity referenced by FK" — here `compose-cov-move-0083` for the
    compose FK and `env-cov-move-0083` for the environment FK.

  - compose-processTemplate is the third POST in the compose/* roster
    sharing the two-required-string-body-fields shape — joining
    API-0078 (compose-import: base64+composeId) and API-0083
    (compose-move: composeId+targetEnvironmentId). Of those three,
    API-0085 is the closer structural twin to API-0078 since BOTH
    pair `base64` with `composeId`; the only operational difference
    is that compose-import creates a compose from a template payload
    while compose-processTemplate processes a template against an
    existing compose. The fixture mirrors that closeness — same field
    names, just a slug swap — keeping the cross-story grep contract
    intact.
  - When the sample fixture would otherwise be a "real" base64 string
    that's expensive to keep in sync, a deterministic placeholder
    (`compose-cov-process-template-0085-payload`) is preferable. It
    keeps the cross-story grep prefix consistent and makes the
    intent visible to anyone reading the file. The harness performs
    no schema validation of the body content — only that the CLI
    forwards it verbatim — so the placeholder convention is wire-
    safe today. A future schema validator might reject non-base64
    in this field; that should be addressed at the harness layer
    (an optional per-case "validateBody" hook) rather than by
    leaking real-looking payloads into fixtures.
  - The compose/* tag now has 17 covered entries spanning the canonical
    shape templates: minimal-composeId-only POST (API-0066/0067/0068/
    0073/0074/0080), full create envelope (API-0069), composeId+
    deleteVolumes (API-0070), composeId+optional-title/description
    POST (API-0071), template envelope (API-0072), single-required-
    query GET (API-0075/0076/0077/0084), POST with two required body
    fields (API-0078/0083/0085 — new), POST with required+optional
    split (API-0079), multi-required-query GET (API-0081), and
    required+optional-enum GET (API-0082). Eight compose/* operations
    remain (processTemplate done; randomizeCompose, redeploy,
    refreshToken, search, start, stop, templates, update).

    suffix, while compose-randomizeCompose randomizes service names

    story grep contract relies on every prior peer being grep-able
    by both operationId and slug; new peers in the same shape family
    should NOT introduce a new comment template — they should cite

    query GET (API-0081), and required+optional-enum GET (API-0082).

    redeploy, refreshToken, search, start, stop, templates, update).

    the compose/* taxonomy (API-0079/0086). When a future compose/*
    POST story declares one required + one or more optional string
    fields, classify it against this peer pair first; only spec-

  - `go test ./...`         → all packages pass (internal/cli ~0.9s)
  - `go test -race ./...`   → all packages pass (internal/cli ~2.5s)
  - Targeted: `go test ./internal/cli -run 'TestAPICoverage' -v -count=1`
    confirms all 5 sub-phases (RegistryInvariants, SchemaCommandWorks,
    ManifestListsOperation, SuccessRoundTrip, RepresentativeFailure) fire and
    PASS for `API-0089/compose-search`.
- Remaining risks / follow-ups: none specific to this story. Future
  compose/* GET stories with all-optional query params (none currently
  pending in the priority-2 queue) can copy this fixture verbatim and just
  rotate the slug.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - When adding a new structural-first fixture (e.g. all-optional query
    params for the first time on a tag), point the comment at the closest
    cross-tag precedent (API-0035 here) — that's the canonical way the
    roster signals "this pattern already exists, just on a different tag".
  - HTTP query strings are untyped on the wire, so OpenAPI `number` query
    params travel through the `--input` JSON `query` field as strings.
    Use canonical decimal grammar (`"10"` / `"0"`, never `"10.0"`) so a
    future schema validator that re-coerces query strings to numbers
    still accepts them — same convention API-0035 established.
  - Don't bloat fixtures into N-key maps when N is large. Pick a
    representative slice (free-text + scoped + numeric pagination = 3
    facets) so the success-leg `r.URL.Query()` re-read still proves
    end-to-end forwarding without making the diff a wall of slugs.
  - The harness's five-phase contract (`TestAPICoverage_*`) ranges over
    `coveredAPIOperations`, so adding a single struct literal is the entire
    surface area of a coverage story — never touch the harness functions
    themselves unless the contract is changing for ALL stories.
---
